+# OpenRouter judge setup
+
+Account, credit and secret changes belong to you. Never paste a key into chat,
+source files, YAML, benchmark arguments or a Git commit.
+
+## Account and credentials
+
+1. USER ACTION NEEDED: Create an account or sign in at <https://openrouter.ai>.
+2. USER ACTION NEEDED: Open [Credits](https://openrouter.ai/settings/credits),
+   buy a small starting balance, for example $5 if offered, and leave automatic
+   top-ups off for the initial experiment.
+3. USER ACTION NEEDED: Open [API keys](https://openrouter.ai/settings/keys),
+   choose **Create API Key**, name it `hill-climb-judges`, and set **Key limit →
+   Credit limit** to $1 for the first check. Leave the reset interval unset for
+   a lifetime limit. Existing keys have the same fields in their edit dialog.
+   OpenRouter documents these controls in [key credit limits](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/best-practices/capping-shared-agent-keys).
+4. USER ACTION NEEDED: Store the key as a Windows *user* environment variable
+   in PowerShell. This masked prompt keeps the value out of shell history:
+
+   ```powershell
+   $routerSecret = Read-Host 'Paste <OPENROUTER_API_KEY> here, never into chat' -AsSecureString
+   $routerCredential = [System.Net.NetworkCredential]::new('', $routerSecret)
+   [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('OPENROUTER_API_KEY', $routerCredential.Password, 'User')
+   $env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY = $routerCredential.Password
+   Remove-Variable routerSecret, routerCredential
+   ```
+
+   Exact literal equivalent, with a placeholder only:
+
+   ```powershell
+   [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('OPENROUTER_API_KEY', '<OPENROUTER_API_KEY>', 'User')
+   ```
+
+   Prefer the masked version so the real value never enters PowerShell history.
+   New shells inherit the user variable. Existing shells can load it with
+   `$env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('OPENROUTER_API_KEY', 'User')`.
+   The variable lives in your Windows profile. Do not print it.
+5. USER ACTION NEEDED: For cloud runs, sign in to your Google account using
+   `gcloud auth login`, then enable Secret Manager if needed:
+
+   ```powershell
+   gcloud services enable secretmanager.googleapis.com --project=hill-climb-sims
+   ```
+
+   Create `openrouter-api-key` from stdin, using the environment variable above:
+
+   ```powershell
+   $env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY | gcloud secrets create openrouter-api-key --project=hill-climb-sims --replication-policy=automatic --data-file=-
+   ```
+
+   The stdin source is `<OPENROUTER_API_KEY>`, not a repository file. PowerShell
+   adds a newline; the retrieval commands below trim it. If the secret already
+   exists, rotate it with
+   `$env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY | gcloud secrets versions add openrouter-api-key --project=hill-climb-sims --data-file=-`.
+   The [gcloud create reference](https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud/reference/secrets/create)
+   documents `--data-file=-` and PowerShell's newline behavior.
+6. USER ACTION NEEDED: Grant only the cloud runner's service account access to
+   this secret. Replace the service-account placeholder with the identity used
+   by the job, then run:
+
+   ```powershell
+   gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding openrouter-api-key --project=hill-climb-sims --member='serviceAccount:<RUNNER_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL>' --role=roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor
+   ```
+
+   In the Linux cloud job, retrieve the value into the process environment before
+   running the benchmark or harness. Keep shell tracing off:
+
+   ```sh
+   set +x
+   export OPENROUTER_API_KEY=[REDACTED]\r\n')"
+   export HARNESS_CLAUDE_BACKEND=openrouter
+   export HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS=google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite
+   ```
+
+   If the job runs in Docker, pass the existing variable by name with
+   `docker run --rm -e OPENROUTER_API_KEY -e HARNESS_CLAUDE_BACKEND -e HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS ...`.
+   The current cluster launcher does not inject this secret automatically. Put
+   retrieval in the cloud runner or job wrapper.
+
+   The labelled station photo is tracked at `tests/data/table_c_topdown.jpg`;
+   include it when packaging the checkout for a cloud job.
+7. USER ACTION NEEDED: Optional, review [Privacy](https://openrouter.ai/settings/privacy)
+   and [Observability](https://openrouter.ai/workspaces/default/observability).
+   Choose whether providers may train on inputs, whether zero data retention is
+   required, and whether prompts and images should appear in logs. Stricter
+   choices can remove providers. See [data collection](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/privacy/data-collection)
+   and [provider routing](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/routing/provider-selection).
+   These benchmarks use the harness's default provider routing.
+
+## Verify and measure
+
+8. USER ACTION NEEDED: From this worktree in PowerShell, run the offline check.
+   It needs no account, key or network:
+
+   ```powershell
+   $judgePython = '..\hill_climb_harness\.venv\Scripts\python.exe'
+   & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py --dry-run --hedge 1 --hedge 2 --out runs/judges-dry
+   ```
+
+   This checks all eight singles and the Haiku/Gemini race on four photo
+   questions and two MuJoCo scenes. `--hedge 2` sets `claude.hedge` to two copies.
+   Every request passes through the real monitor and shared wire-body builder;
+   image bytes, text, ordering, system prompt and token limit are checked.
+   Dry summaries leave accuracy, latency and cost null. Add `--no-sim` for a
+   photo-only check if the local `yam_mujoco` example, MuJoCo or Pillow is missing.
+   Full runs require the same renderer as `tools/check_monitor.py --sim`.
+9. USER ACTION NEEDED: When ready to spend credits, run one single-model check:
+
+   ```powershell
+   & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py --judge openrouter:google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite -n 1 --max-cost-usd 0.10 --out runs/judges-first
+   ```
+
+   Look for numeric cost and parsed answers. Exit 1 means wrong answers;
+   exit 2 means setup, validation, accounting, budget or interruption stopped
+   the run. Partial reports are preserved. Reports omit reply text and exception
+   messages. HTTP and parse errors count as incorrect stage answers.
+10. USER ACTION NEEDED: Compare singles, a model race, and a hedge after the
+    first check works:
+
+    ```powershell
+    & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py -n 3 --hedge 1 --hedge 2 --max-cost-usd 1 --out runs/judges-all
+    & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py --judge openrouter:anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5,google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite -n 3 --max-cost-usd 0.50 --out runs/judges-race
+    ```
+
+    Linux cloud jobs use `python tools/bench_judges.py` with the same arguments.
+    For a harness run after model selection, set `HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS` to
+    one ID or a comma-separated race, and pass `--claude-backend openrouter` to
+    the existing harness command. A race uses the first nonempty response, even
+    if it later fails parsing. A hedge uses the first parseable monitor answer.
+    Neither checks correctness before choosing a winner.
+
+`summary.csv` and `results.json` report stage and guard accuracy, parse and
+transport failure rates, and latency p50, p90 and max. Guard accuracy uses only
+guard-labelled cases. `accepted_done_accuracy` also applies the confidence gate.
+Percentiles use linear interpolation. JSON includes each trial, plan hash,
+frame digest and numeric usage. Connector close-ups have no stage/guard labels.
+
+Winning latency ends when `check_now` returns. Accounting waits separately for
+every losing HTTP call before starting the next trial. `cost_per_call_usd` is
+total reported cost divided by HTTP attempts; `cost_per_trial_usd` includes
+every race and hedge copy for one verdict. [Usage accounting](https://openrouter.ai/docs/cookbook/administration/usage-accounting)
+returns `usage.cost` automatically. Missing costs stay null and stop scheduling.
+`--max-cost-usd` defaults to $1 and stops new trials at reported spend. It can
+overshoot by the full concurrent trial already started. Zero starts no calls.
+The key's server-side credit limit is the separate account control.
+
+`--judge api:claude-haiku-5-5` and `--judge subscription:claude-haiku-5-5` also
+work with their existing credentials, but these transports do not expose dollar
+cost in usage. A live run stops after the first trial with unknown cost. Use
+OpenRouter for the budgeted comparison. No live benchmark was run during
+implementation.
+
+## Default candidates
+
+The unauthenticated [live model catalog](https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models)
+was checked on 2026-11-08. All eight advertise image input and text output.
+Prices below are advertised USD per million input/output tokens, not measured
+costs. Image tokens, provider choice, caching and reasoning change actual cost.
+Speed and guard reliability are hypotheses to measure.
+
+| OpenRouter ID | Input / output | Reason to include |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5` | $0.10 / $0.50 | Existing judge family, comparison baseline |
+| `google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite` | $0.25 / $1.50 | Flash-Lite alternative and default race partner |
+| `qwen/qwen3.8-flash` | $0.15 / $0.47 | Current Qwen Flash with vision, inexpensive output |
+| `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` | $0.0335 / $0.083 | Cheapest advertised paid Flash candidate here |
+| `meta-llama/llama-4-scout` | $0.10 / $0.30 | Independent vision model family at low cost |
+| `openai/gpt-5-nano` | $0.05 / $0.40 | Low-cost small vision model; check reasoning delay |
+| `mistralai/mistral-small-2603` | $0.15 / $0.60 | Current Mistral Small vision alternative |
+| `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` | $0.0675 / $0.225 | Low-cost sparse Gemma vision alternative |
+
+Text-only GLM 4.7 Flash and Qwen Coder Flash are excluded. Batch endpoints,
+image-generation variants and free variants are excluded from the defaults.
+The six cases are a smoke test, not evidence to deploy a guard. Compare guard
+false negatives and false positives separately, collect more labelled match
+frames, and increase repeats before choosing a judge for a match.
diff --git a/harness/cable_end.py b/harness/cable_end.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..848f658
--- /dev/null
+++ b/harness/cable_end.py
@@ -0,0 +1,163 @@
+"""Which end of the orange cable is where: USB-C end, USB-A end, or neither, from any camera frame.
+
+Two steps, numpy + OpenCV only (no torch at runtime):
+1. Candidates: compact orange blobs, i.e. the anodised ferrules (the cable is a long thin run, the strain-relief
+   ribs are slivers). Skin, faces and the orange golf ball also pass; step 2 rejects them.
+2. A small CNN (MobileNetV3-small, 96 x 96 crop, ~6 MB ONNX run by cv2.dnn) labels each crop usb_c / usb_a / none.
+   Trained on Blender renders of this cable (tools/cable_end/render_cable.py: random pose, lighting, lens,
+   backgrounds from station frames) plus hand-labelled station crops (tools/cable_end/train.py).
+
+The station's cables are USB-A to USB-C; the USB-A end is the one that goes into the power strip's USB ports.
+Used to draw "USB-C end" / "USB-A end" boxes on the frames the monitor sees, like harness.detect's boxes. It never
+touches Dream's inputs or the prompts.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+
+LABELS = ("usb_c", "usb_a", "none")
+NAMES = {"usb_c": "USB-C end", "usb_a": "USB-A end"}
+WORK_W = 640
+SIZE = 96
+BATCH = 8                 # fixed DNN input batch (see CableEndClassifier._forward)
+CROP_SCALE = 2.6
+MEAN = np.array([0.485, 0.456, 0.406], np.float32)
+STD = np.array([0.229, 0.224, 0.225], np.float32)
+
+
+@dataclass
+class End:
+    label: str            # usb_c | usb_a
+    prob: float
+    x0: float
+    y0: float
+    x1: float
+    y1: float             # fractions of the image
+
+
+def ferrules(bgr: np.ndarray, work_w: int = WORK_W) -> list[dict]:
+    """Candidate ferrules: [{cx, cy, side}] in the input image's pixels (side = crop side)."""
+    h0, w0 = bgr.shape[:2]
+    s = work_w / w0
+    im = cv2.resize(bgr, (work_w, max(1, round(h0 * s))), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
+    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(im, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
+    H, S, V = hsv[..., 0], hsv[..., 1], hsv[..., 2]
+    mask = ((H >= 3) & (H <= 22) & (S >= 110) & (V >= 60)).astype(np.uint8)
+    mask = cv2.morphologyEx(mask, cv2.MORPH_CLOSE, np.ones((3, 3), np.uint8))
+    n, lab, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(mask)
+    total = im.shape[0] * im.shape[1]
+    out = []
+    for i in range(1, n):
+        area = int(stats[i, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA])
+        if area < 30 or area > 0.08 * total:
+            continue
+        ys, xs = np.nonzero(lab == i)
+        (cx, cy), (w, h), _ = cv2.minAreaRect(np.stack([xs, ys], 1).astype(np.float32))
+        L, W = max(w, h), max(1.0, min(w, h))
+        if L / W > 4.0 or area / max(L * W, 1.0) < 0.5:
+            continue
+        out.append({"cx": cx / s, "cy": cy / s, "side": max(L * CROP_SCALE, 24) / s})
+    return out
+
+
+def crop(bgr: np.ndarray, cand: dict, size: int = SIZE) -> np.ndarray:
+    side = cand["side"]
+    x0, y0 = cand["cx"] - side / 2, cand["cy"] - side / 2
+    M = np.float32([[size / side, 0, -x0 * size / side], [0, size / side, -y0 * size / side]])
+    return cv2.warpAffine(bgr, M, (size, size), flags=cv2.INTER_AREA, borderMode=cv2.BORDER_REPLICATE)
+
+
+def _prep(crops: list[np.ndarray]) -> np.ndarray:
+    x = np.stack([c[:, :, ::-1].astype(np.float32) / 255.0 for c in crops])
+    return ((x - MEAN) / STD).transpose(0, 3, 1, 2).copy()
+
+
+class CableEndClassifier:
+    def __init__(self, model_path: str | Path, min_prob: float = 0.7, max_candidates: int = 24):
+        self.net = cv2.dnn.readNetFromONNX(str(model_path))
+        self.min_prob = min_prob
+        self.max_candidates = max_candidates
+
+    def _forward(self, x: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+        """Always the same input shape, padded: OpenCV 5.0's DNN kills the process when one net is reused with a
+        batch larger than an earlier one (2, 5, 4, 4, then 16 crashed it, 7 Nov). Chunks of BATCH cost ~2.5 ms."""
+        out = []
+        for k in range(0, len(x), BATCH):
+            chunk = x[k:k + BATCH]
+            pad = np.zeros((BATCH,) + x.shape[1:], np.float32)
+            pad[:len(chunk)] = chunk
+            self.net.setInput(pad)
+            out.append(self.net.forward()[:len(chunk)].copy())
+        return np.concatenate(out)
+
+    def ends(self, bgr: np.ndarray) -> list[End]:
+        cands = ferrules(bgr)[: self.max_candidates]
+        if not cands:
+            return []
+        logits = self._forward(_prep([crop(bgr, c) for c in cands]))
+        e = np.exp(logits - logits.max(1, keepdims=True))
+        probs = e / e.sum(1, keepdims=True)
+        h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
+        out = []
+        for c, p in zip(cands, probs):
+            k = int(p.argmax())
+            if LABELS[k] == "none" or p[k] < self.min_prob:
+                continue
+            half = c["side"] / 2.6 * 0.75     # box around the ferrule and tip, not the whole crop
+            out.append(End(LABELS[k], float(p[k]), max(0, c["cx"] - half) / w, max(0, c["cy"] - half) / h,
+                           min(w, c["cx"] + half) / w, min(h, c["cy"] + half) / h))
+        return out
+
+    def ends_jpeg(self, jpeg: bytes) -> list[End]:
+        bgr = cv2.imdecode(np.frombuffer(jpeg, np.uint8), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
+        return [] if bgr is None else self.ends(bgr)
+
+    def annotate(self, jpeg: bytes) -> bytes:
+        bgr = cv2.imdecode(np.frombuffer(jpeg, np.uint8), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
+        if bgr is None:
+            return jpeg
+        found = self.ends(bgr)
+        if not found:
+            return jpeg
+        h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
+        th = max(2, w // 300)
+        for e in found:
+            col = (255, 0, 255) if e.label == "usb_c" else (0, 200, 255)   # magenta = USB-C, amber = USB-A
+            p0, p1 = (int(e.x0 * w), int(e.y0 * h)), (int(e.x1 * w), int(e.y1 * h))
+            cv2.rectangle(bgr, p0, p1, col, th)
+            cv2.putText(bgr, f"{NAMES[e.label]} {e.prob:.2f}", (p0[0], max(12, p0[1] - 4)), 0,
+                        max(0.4, w / 1600), col, max(1, th // 2))
+        ok, buf = cv2.imencode(".jpg", bgr, [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 88])
+        return buf.tobytes() if ok else jpeg
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def legend() -> str:
+        return ("MAGENTA boxes = USB-C end of a cable, AMBER boxes = USB-A end (the end that plugs into the power "
+                "strip's USB ports); from a local classifier, can miss or mislabel.")
+
+
+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
+    import argparse
+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="label cable ends in images")
+    ap.add_argument("model")
+    ap.add_argument("images", nargs="+")
+    ap.add_argument("--out-dir", default=None)
+    a = ap.parse_args(argv)
+    clf = CableEndClassifier(a.model)
+    for p in a.images:
+        data = Path(p).read_bytes()
+        for e in clf.ends_jpeg(data):
+            print(f"{p}: {NAMES[e.label]} p={e.prob:.2f} box=({e.x0:.2f},{e.y0:.2f})-({e.x1:.2f},{e.y1:.2f})")
+        if a.out_dir:
+            Path(a.out_dir).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+            (Path(a.out_dir) / (Path(p).stem + "_ends.jpg")).write_bytes(clf.annotate(data))
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

+def match_problems(args: argparse.Namespace, plan) -> list[str]:

@@ -0,0 +1,337 @@
+"""The runner's decision tree as weighted equations, so its constants can be tuned (HARNESS_DECISION_MODEL section 5).
+
+Three pieces, all pure functions of numbers (no I/O, no clock):
+
+1. Done evidence, a running score over looks:  l_t = keep * l_{t-1} + sum_i w_i * phi_i(t),  done when l_t >= theta.
+   With DEFAULT weights it is exactly the runner's rule: `confirm` monitor "done" looks in a row pass the stage, a
+   "not done" look resets the count, a repeated look at the same frames counts for nothing, and a second "done"
+   within done_window_s passes even with a "failing" flicker in between.
+2. Fail score:  F = combine_k(w_k * g_k), each g_k a ratio that reaches 1 when today's hard rule fires
+   (consecutive failing looks / confirm, consecutive "stalled" looks / confirm, time since progress rose / stall_s,
+   elapsed / timeout_s). A look is failing on a no, a "failing" status, or progress falling REGRESSION_DROP below
+   the attempt's best (look_failing). The attempt fails when F >= theta_fail. combine="max" is today's tree (any one rule);
+   combine="sum" lets weak signals add up, which is what tuning can explore.
+3. Value of the time left:  W_i(L) = max_T  F_i(T) (V_i + W_{i+1}(L - t_succ)) + (1 - F_i(T)) * [skip ? W_{i+1}(L - T) : 0]
+   over a stage list, by dynamic programming on a 1 s grid. It gives each stage the timeout that maximises expected
+   points for the time left (`plan_timeouts`) and the value of an action (`action_value`):
+   Q(a) = P_a * V_s + W(L - c_a) - risk_weight * R_a.
+
+Nothing here moves a robot or changes the runner yet: the runner keeps its own rules. The tests check that the
+default weights reproduce them; tuning and shadow logging come next.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import math
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace
+from typing import Iterable, Sequence
+
+# Today's runner constants (harness/runner.py), the starting point for tuning.
+CONFIRM = 2
+DONE_WINDOW_S = 15.0
+REGRESSION_DROP = 0.3
+FAIL_MIN_FRACTION = 0.5
+
+
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 1. done evidence
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class DoneWeights:
+    w_monitor: float = 1.0          # a monitor "done" look
+    w_sensor_release: float = 0.0   # gripper went from closed on an object to open near the target (/arms); new, off
+    w_goal_box: float = 0.0         # detector sees the object at its goal (in the bin, on the tower); new, off
+    w_plug_count: float = 0.0       # cable-end boxes: one fewer plug in the strip; new, off
+    keep: float = 1.0               # share of the score carried to the next look when nothing argues against it
+    reset_on_no: bool = True        # a "not done" / "failing" look sets the score to 0 (outside the done window)
+    theta: float = float(CONFIRM)   # done when the score reaches this
+    window_s: float = DONE_WINDOW_S  # a second "done" within this passes, flickers in between notwithstanding
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Look:
+    """One check. done: the verifier's answer (True / False / None = no answer). failing: monitor status failing."""
+    t: float
+    done: bool | None
+    failing: bool = False
+    same_frames: bool = False       # the same camera images as the previous done look
+    monitor: bool = True            # from the monitor: a look with no done answer still resets the count
+    sensor_release: float = 0.0     # 0..1 features from the fast sensors
+    goal_box: float = 0.0
+    plug_count: float = 0.0
+
+
+@dataclass
+class DoneState:
+    score: float = 0.0
+    last_done_t: float | None = None
+
+
+def done_step(state: DoneState, look: Look, w: DoneWeights = DoneWeights()) -> tuple[DoneState, bool]:
+    """Folds one look into the done score. Returns (new state, stage passes now)."""
+    fast = w.w_sensor_release * look.sensor_release + w.w_goal_box * look.goal_box + w.w_plug_count * look.plug_count
+    if look.done is True and look.same_frames:
+        return DoneState(state.score * w.keep + fast, state.last_done_t), state.score * w.keep + fast >= w.theta
+    if look.done is True:
+        recent = state.last_done_t is not None and look.t - state.last_done_t <= w.window_s
+        score = state.score * w.keep + w.w_monitor + fast
+        return DoneState(score, look.t), score >= w.theta or recent
+    # The runner: a no / failing look right after a done (within the window) changes nothing; otherwise it resets the
+    # count. A monitor look with no done answer resets it even inside the window (the window still passes the next done).
+    flicker = look.done is False or look.failing
+    blank = look.done is None and look.monitor
+    in_window = state.last_done_t is not None and look.t - state.last_done_t <= w.window_s
+    if flicker and in_window:
+        score = state.score
+    elif (flicker or blank) and w.reset_on_no:
+        score = fast
+    elif flicker or blank:
+        score = state.score * w.keep + fast
+    else:
+        score = state.score * w.keep + fast
+    return DoneState(score, state.last_done_t), score >= w.theta
+
+
+def run_looks(looks: Iterable[Look], w: DoneWeights = DoneWeights()) -> float | None:
+    """Time of the look that passes the stage, or None."""
+    st = DoneState()
+    for lk in looks:
+        st, ok = done_step(st, lk, w)
+        if ok:
+            return lk.t
+    return None
+
+
+# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 2. fail score
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class FailWeights:
+    w_fail: float = 1.0          # consecutive failing looks / confirm (monitor: only after min_fraction of the timeout)
+    w_stalled: float = 1.0       # consecutive "stalled" monitor looks / confirm (stages with a stall window)
+    w_stall: float = 1.0         # seconds since progress last rose / stall_s
+    w_time: float = 1.0          # elapsed / timeout_s
+    w_on_target: float = 0.0     # subtracted while a gripper holds the target (today: a one-off 20 s extension)
+    w_regress: float = 1.0       # in look_failing: (max progress - progress) / regress_drop >= 1 makes a failing look
+    confirm: int = CONFIRM
+    regress_drop: float = REGRESSION_DROP
+    min_fraction: float = FAIL_MIN_FRACTION
+    combine: str = "max"         # "max" = today's tree (any rule fires); "sum" = weak signals add up
+    theta: float = 1.0
+
+
+def look_failing(done: bool | None, status_failing: bool, progress: float | None, max_progress: float | None,
+                 done_seen: bool, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> bool:
+    """Is one look a failing look? The verifier said no, the monitor said failing, or progress fell regress_drop
+    below the attempt's best (not counted once a done has been seen, as in the runner)."""
+    regress = 0.0
+    if progress is not None and max_progress is not None and not done_seen:
+        regress = w.w_regress * max(0.0, max_progress - progress) / w.regress_drop
+    return done is False or status_failing or regress >= 1.0 - 1e-9
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class AttemptState:
+    elapsed: float
+    timeout_s: float
+    consec_fail: int = 0                 # failing looks in a row (look_failing)
+    consec_stalled: int = 0              # "stalled" monitor looks in a row
+    since_rise_s: float | None = None    # seconds since progress last rose by STALL_RISE (None: no progress yet)
+    stall_s: float | None = None         # the stage's stall window (None: no stall rules)
+    monitor: bool = True                 # failing looks come from the monitor (then min_fraction applies)
+    on_target: bool = False
+
+
+def fail_terms(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> dict[str, float]:
+    """Each term reaches its weight when its hard rule would fire on its own."""
+    gate_ok = not a.monitor or a.elapsed >= w.min_fraction * a.timeout_s
+    terms = {"fail": w.w_fail * (a.consec_fail / w.confirm) if gate_ok else 0.0,
+             "time": w.w_time * a.elapsed / a.timeout_s}
+    if a.stall_s is not None:
+        terms["stalled"] = w.w_stalled * a.consec_stalled / w.confirm
+        if a.since_rise_s is not None:
+            terms["stall"] = w.w_stall * a.since_rise_s / a.stall_s
+    return terms
+
+
+def fail_score(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> float:
+    terms = list(fail_terms(a, w).values())
+    s = max(terms) if w.combine == "max" else sum(terms)
+    return s - (w.w_on_target if a.on_target else 0.0)
+
+
+def attempt_fails(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> bool:
+    return fail_score(a, w) >= w.theta - 1e-9
+
+
+# -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 3. value of the time left
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class StageModel:
+    """One stage for planning. Success by time tau: F(tau) = p_max * (1 - exp(-max(0, tau - t_min) / tau_c)).
+    `gate`: a pass bar; if it fails, nothing after it scores (it is retried until the clock instead of skipped).
+    Fit p_max, t_min, tau_c per stage from run logs or the stress sim."""
+    name: str
+    value: float
+    p_max: float
+    tau_c: float
+    t_min: float = 0.0
+    gate: bool = False
+
+    def F(self, tau: float) -> float:
+        if tau <= self.t_min:
+            return 0.0
+        return self.p_max * (1.0 - math.exp(-(tau - self.t_min) / self.tau_c))
+
+    def mean_success_time(self, T: float, step: float = 1.0) -> float:
+        """Expected time spent given success by T (integral of tau dF over [0, T] / F(T))."""
+        FT = self.F(T)
+        if FT <= 0:
+            return T
+        n = max(1, int(T / step))
+        acc, prev = 0.0, 0.0
+        for k in range(1, n + 1):
+            tau = k * T / n
+            f = self.F(tau)
+            acc += (tau - T / n / 2) * (f - prev)
+            prev = f
+        return acc / FT
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Plan:
+    """W[i][L]: expected points from stage i on with L whole seconds left; best_T[i][L]: that stage's timeout."""
+    stages: Sequence[StageModel]
+    horizon_s: int
+    W: list[list[float]] = field(default_factory=list)
+    best_T: list[list[int]] = field(default_factory=list)
+
+    def value(self, i: int, left_s: float) -> float:
+        if i >= len(self.stages):
+            return 0.0
+        return self.W[i][max(0, min(self.horizon_s, int(left_s)))]
+
+    def timeout(self, i: int, left_s: float) -> int:
+        return self.best_T[i][max(0, min(self.horizon_s, int(left_s)))]
+
+
+def plan_timeouts(stages: Sequence[StageModel], horizon_s: int, step_s: int = 1) -> Plan:
+    """Backward DP over the stage list. For a gate stage a failure leaves 0 (nothing later scores)."""
+    n = len(stages)
+    W = [[0.0] * (horizon_s + 1) for _ in range(n + 1)]
+    best = [[0] * (horizon_s + 1) for _ in range(n)]
+    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
+        st = stages[i]
+        succ_t = [st.mean_success_time(T) for T in range(horizon_s + 1)]
+        for L in range(horizon_s + 1):
+            top, top_T = W[i + 1][L], 0           # T = 0: skip the stage
+            for T in range(step_s, L + 1, step_s):
+                p = st.F(T)
+                after_ok = W[i + 1][max(0, L - int(round(succ_t[T])))]
+                after_fail = 0.0 if st.gate else W[i + 1][L - T]
+                v = p * (st.value + after_ok) + (1 - p) * after_fail
+                if v > top + 1e-12:
+                    top, top_T = v, T
+            W[i][L], best[i][L] = top, top_T
+    return Plan(stages, horizon_s, W, best)
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Action:
+    """A choice at a decision point: p = chance it succeeds the stage, cost_s = seconds it uses, risk = 0..1 chance of
+    something costly (a DQ, a knocked tower, an illegal ball)."""
+    name: str
+    p: float
+    cost_s: float
+    risk: float = 0.0
+
+
+def action_value(plan: Plan, i: int, left_s: float, a: Action, risk_weight: float = 50.0) -> float:
+    """Q(a) = p (V_i + W_{i+1}(L - c)) + (1 - p) W_i'(L - c) - risk_weight * risk, where after a failed action the
+    same stage can still be tried with the time left (W_i), or nothing later scores for a gate that ran out."""
+    st = plan.stages[i]
+    rest = left_s - a.cost_s
+    if rest < 0:
+        return -risk_weight * a.risk
+    ok = a.p * (st.value + plan.value(i + 1, rest))
+    again = (1 - a.p) * plan.value(i, rest)
+    return ok + again - risk_weight * a.risk
+
+
+def choose(plan: Plan, i: int, left_s: float, actions: Sequence[Action], risk_weight: float = 50.0) -> Action:
+    return max(actions, key=lambda a: action_value(plan, i, left_s, a, risk_weight))
+
+
+def with_weights(obj, **kw):
+    """Copy of a weights dataclass with some fields changed (for hill climbing)."""
+    return replace(obj, **kw)
+
+
+# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 4. shadow mode
+def weights_from(raw: dict | None) -> tuple[DoneWeights, FailWeights]:
+    """plan `decide: {done: {...}, fail: {...}}` -> weights. Unknown keys are an error (a typo must not pass)."""
+    raw = raw or {}
+    extra = set(raw) - {"done", "fail"}
+    if extra:
+        raise ValueError(f"plan.decide: unknown key(s): {', '.join(sorted(extra))}")
+    out = []
+    for key, cls in (("done", DoneWeights), ("fail", FailWeights)):
+        part = raw.get(key) or {}
+        if not isinstance(part, dict):
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.decide.{key}: must be a mapping")
+        names = set(cls.__dataclass_fields__)
+        bad = set(part) - names
+        if bad:
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.decide.{key}: unknown weight(s): {', '.join(sorted(bad))}")
+        if key == "fail" and part.get("combine", "max") not in ("max", "sum"):
+            raise ValueError("plan.decide.fail.combine: max or sum")
+        out.append(cls(**part))
+    return out[0], out[1]
+
+
+class Shadow:
+    """Runs the equations next to the runner on every look and reports what they would decide. It never acts.
+
+    The runner calls start() at each attempt and look() once per verdict, with the inputs it already computed
+    (`failing` includes the regression rule). Counts follow the runner's: a failing look right after a done (within
+    the window) is not counted, a done resets the failing count."""
+
+    def __init__(self, done: DoneWeights = DoneWeights(), fail: FailWeights = FailWeights(), *,
+                 fixed: set[str] = frozenset()):
+        self.base_done, self.base_fail = done, fail
+        self.done_w, self.fail_w = done, fail
+        self._fixed = set(fixed)        # "done.theta", "fail.confirm": set by the plan, not taken from the stage
+        self._st = DoneState()
+        self._consec_fail = 0
+        self._timeout = 1.0
+        self._stall: float | None = None
+
+    @classmethod
+    def from_plan(cls, raw: dict | None) -> "Shadow":
+        done, fail = weights_from(raw)
+        raw = raw or {}
+        fixed = {f"{k}.{n}" for k in ("done", "fail") for n in (raw.get(k) or {})}
+        return cls(done, fail, fixed=fixed)
+
+    def start(self, timeout_s: float, stall_s: float | None, confirm: int = CONFIRM) -> None:
+        """Per attempt. The stage's confirm sets the done threshold and the failing-look count unless the plan fixed them."""
+        self.done_w = self.base_done if "done.theta" in self._fixed else replace(self.base_done, theta=float(confirm))
+        self.fail_w = self.base_fail if "fail.confirm" in self._fixed else replace(self.base_fail, confirm=int(confirm))
+        self._st, self._consec_fail = DoneState(), 0
+        self._timeout, self._stall = timeout_s, stall_s
+
+    def look(self, elapsed: float, done: bool | None, failing: bool, *, same_frames: bool, monitor: bool,
+             consec_stalled: int, since_rise_s: float | None, sensor_release: float = 0.0, goal_box: float = 0.0,
+             plug_count: float = 0.0) -> dict:
+        lk = Look(elapsed, done, failing=failing and done is not False, same_frames=same_frames, monitor=monitor,
+                  sensor_release=sensor_release, goal_box=goal_box, plug_count=plug_count)
+        in_window = self._st.last_done_t is not None and elapsed - self._st.last_done_t <= self.done_w.window_s
+        self._st, passes = done_step(self._st, lk, self.done_w)
+        if done is True and not same_frames:
+            self._consec_fail = 0
+        elif (failing or done is False) and not in_window:
+            self._consec_fail += 1
+        elif not (failing or done is False) and (done is not None or monitor):
+            self._consec_fail = 0
+        a = AttemptState(elapsed=elapsed, timeout_s=self._timeout, consec_fail=self._consec_fail,
+                         consec_stalled=consec_stalled, since_rise_s=since_rise_s, stall_s=self._stall, monitor=monitor)
+        terms = fail_terms(a, self.fail_w)
+        score = fail_score(a, self.fail_w)
+        return {"done_score": round(self._st.score, 3), "would_pass": bool(passes),
+                "fail_score": round(score, 3), "would_fail": bool(not passes and score >= self.fail_w.theta - 1e-9),
+                "terms": {k: round(v, 3) for k, v in terms.items()}}

+def _ge(x: np.ndarray, t: int) -> np.ndarray | None:
+    """x >= t on a uint8 plane; None means true everywhere (no array built)."""
+    return None if t <= 0 else (np.zeros(x.shape, bool) if t > 255 else x >= t)
+
+
+def _le(x: np.ndarray, t: int) -> np.ndarray | None:
+    return None if t >= 255 else (np.zeros(x.shape, bool) if t < 0 else x <= t)
+
+
+def _and(parts: list[np.ndarray | None], shape: tuple[int, ...]) -> np.ndarray:
+    out = None
+    for p in parts:
+        if p is not None:
+            if out is None:
+                out = p           # a fresh array from _ge/_le: safe to AND into in place
+            else:
+                out &= p
+    return np.ones(shape, bool) if out is None else out
+
+
+def _in_range(hsv: tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray], lo: tuple[int, int, int],
+              hi: tuple[int, int, int]) -> np.ndarray:
+    """lo <= pixel <= hi per channel on the uint8 planes; a hue range with lo > hi wraps around (red). Bounds that
+    every 0-255 value passes build no array (black's hue and saturation, say)."""
+    h, s, v = hsv
+    if lo[0] <= hi[0]:
+        hue = [_ge(h, lo[0]), _le(h, hi[0])]
+    else:
+        a, b = _ge(h, lo[0]), _le(h, hi[0])
+        hue = [None if a is None or b is None else a | b]
+    return _and(hue + [_ge(s, lo[1]), _le(s, hi[1]), _ge(v, lo[2]), _le(v, hi[2])], h.shape)
+
+
+def _close2(m: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+    """scipy.ndimage.binary_closing(m, iterations=2) with its default cross and border 0, in whole-array shifts:
+    two cross dilations, then two cross erosions that also clear the pixels next to the image edge."""
+    for _ in range(2):
+        o = m.copy()
+        o[1:] |= m[:-1]
+        o[:-1] |= m[1:]
+        o[:, 1:] |= m[:, :-1]
+        o[:, :-1] |= m[:, 1:]
+        m = o
+    for _ in range(2):
+        o = m.copy()
+        o[1:] &= m[:-1]
+        o[:-1] &= m[1:]
+        o[:, 1:] &= m[:, :-1]
+        o[:, :-1] &= m[:, 1:]
+        o[0] = o[-1] = False
+        o[:, 0] = o[:, -1] = False
+        m = o
+    return m
+
+
+def _fill_holes(m: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+    """scipy.ndimage.binary_fill_holes(m): background regions (4-connected) that touch no image edge become
+    foreground. One labelling of the background instead of scipy's iterated dilation."""
+    lab, n = _ndi.label(~m)
+    if n == 0:
+        return m
+    edge = np.zeros(n + 1, bool)
+    for line in (lab[0], lab[-1], lab[:, 0], lab[:, -1]):
+        edge[line] = True
+    edge[0] = False                       # label 0 is the foreground itself
+    if edge[1:].all():
+        return m                          # every background region reaches the edge: no holes
+    return ~edge[lab]
+
+
+def _crop(m: np.ndarray) -> tuple[int, int, int, int] | None:
+    """(y0, y1, x0, x1): the box around m's foreground plus one pixel (cut at the image edge), or None if m is empty.
+
+    Filling holes and labelling inside this box give what they give on the whole image: every blob lies inside it,
+    and the one-pixel margin (or the image edge) is background joined to everything outside the box, so a background
+    region reaches the box edge exactly when it reaches the image edge. Blobs keep their raster order, so labels
+    are numbered the same."""
+    rows = np.flatnonzero(m.any(axis=1))
+    if rows.size == 0:
+        return None
+    cols = np.flatnonzero(m.any(axis=0))
+    H, W = m.shape
+    return max(0, rows[0] - 1), min(H, rows[-1] + 2), max(0, cols[0] - 1), min(W, cols[-1] + 2)

+def _candidates(lab: np.ndarray, n: int, spec: ClassSpec, keep: tuple[float, ...] | None, W: int, H: int,

+            continue

+

+def _kept(keep: tuple[float, ...], frac: float, x0: int, x1: int, y0: int, y1: int, W: int, H: int) -> bool:
+    """held()'s size and grip-region test on a blob's box (x1, y1 exclusive), with Box.cx / Box.cy's arithmetic."""
+    cx, cy = (x0 / W + x1 / W) / 2, (y0 / H + y1 / H) / 2
+    return frac >= keep[0] and keep[1] <= cx <= keep[3] and keep[2] <= cy <= keep[4]
+
+
+def _blobs(hsv: tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray], spec: ClassSpec,
+           keep: tuple[float, float, float, float, float] | None = None) -> list[Box]:
+    """The boxes of one colour class, in label order. `keep` = (min_frac, x0, y0, x1, y1): skip, before the shape
+    test, any blob smaller than min_frac or whose box centre is outside the region (the guard's grip_roi): held()
+    would drop those anyway, and the shape test is the costly part per blob."""
+    H, W = hsv[0].shape
+    mask = _in_range(hsv, spec.hsv_lo, spec.hsv_hi)
+    oy = ox = 0
+    if _ndi is not None:   # close specular highlights and the cubes' holes, so shapes read whole
+        mask = _close2(mask)
+        box = _crop(mask)

+    lab, n = _label(mask)
+    if n == 0:

+    out: list[Box] = []
+    for _i, ys, xs, frac in _candidates(lab, n, spec, keep, W, H, oy, ox):
+        kind, orient = _kind(ys, xs)
+        if kind == "cube" and spec.small_ball and frac <= SMALL_BALL_FRAC:

+                            # (the size cap keeps the white bin out; a held ball fills the wrist view and is round)
+        if kind is None or (spec.kinds and kind not in spec.kinds):
+            continue
+        role = spec.kinds.get(kind, "info")
+        label = f"{spec.label or spec.name} {kind}"
+        out.append(Box(label, xs.min() / W, ys.min() / H, (xs.max() + 1) / W, (ys.max() + 1) / H, frac,
+                       orient, role))
     return out

+"""The fast loop: perception + observer + state-space decisions at `rate_hz` (default 20), in its own thread.
+
+Each tick:
+  1. grab the newest live frames (GET /frame/<cam>.jpg, ~4 ms on the station) at a small width,
+  2. run the local classifiers on frames it has not judged before (colour detector guard on the wrists),
+  3. read the gripper sensors (GET /arms) every few ticks,
+  4. update the observer (fast measurements), and fold in any Claude verdict the runner handed over since the last
+     tick (slow measurements),
+  5. step the state-space model and publish the decision.
+
+mode "shadow" (default): logs what it would decide; the runner keeps its own rules.
+mode "live": a guard decision sends /stop at once (like the live guard), and pass / fail / stall are picked up by
+the runner on its next look (check_every_s can be 0.25 s), so a stage ends within one runner look of the evidence.
+The runner's own rules still run: whichever fires first ends the attempt.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import hashlib
+import threading
+import time
+from typing import Any, Callable
+
+from .statespace import Observer, StateSpace, Weights
+
+
+class FastLoop:
+    def __init__(self, *, weights: Weights | None = None, rate_hz: float = 20.0, mode: str = "shadow",
+                 frames: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]] | None = None,
+                 guard_judge: Callable[[dict[str, bytes]], tuple[bool | None, str]] | None = None,
+                 grippers: Callable[[], dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
+                 stop: Callable[[], Any] | None = None, log: Callable[..., None] | None = None,
+                 clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
+        if mode not in ("shadow", "live"):
+            raise ValueError("fast.mode: shadow or live")
+        self.w = weights or Weights()
+        self.ss = StateSpace(self.w)
+        self.obs = Observer(self.w)
+        self.period = 1.0 / rate_hz
+        self.mode = mode
+        self._frames, self._judge, self._grippers, self._stop = frames, guard_judge, grippers, stop
+        self._log = log or (lambda *a, **k: None)
+        self._clock = clock
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()
+        self._pending: list[dict[str, float]] = []      # verdict inputs handed over by the runner
+        self._sig: dict[str, str] = {}
+        self._stage: str | None = None
+        self._guarded = False
+        self._last_t: float | None = None
+        self.decision: dict[str, bool] = {}
+        self.tripped: str | None = None
+        self.ticks = 0
+        self.tick_ms: list[float] = []
+        self._closed = threading.Event()
+        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None
+
+    # -- runner hooks -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def attempt_start(self, stage: str, timeout_s: float, stall_s: float | None, *, guarded: bool,
+                      monitor: bool = True) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            self._stage, self._guarded = stage, guarded
+            self.ss.start(timeout_s, stall_s, monitor=monitor)
+            self.obs.reset()
+            self._pending.clear()
+            self._sig.clear()
+            self._last_t = self._clock()
+            self.decision, self.tripped = {}, None
+
+    def attempt_end(self) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            self._stage = None
+
+    def verdict(self, *, done: bool | None, failing: bool, same_frames: bool, stalled: bool,
+                progress: float | None, guard: bool | None, holding: bool | None = None) -> None:
+        """A real monitor verdict landed (slow measurement). Applied on the next tick."""
+        with self._lock:
+            self._pending.append(dict(done=done, failing=failing, same=same_frames, stalled=stalled,
+                                      progress=progress, guard=guard, holding=holding))
+
+    # -- one tick -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def step(self) -> dict[str, bool]:
+        t0 = self._clock()
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._stage is None:
+                return {}
+            pending, self._pending = self._pending, []
+            stage, guarded = self._stage, self._guarded
+            dt = t0 - (self._last_t or t0)
+            self._last_t = t0
+        l_yes = l_no = 0.0
+        if guarded and self._frames is not None and self._judge is not None:
+            for role, jpeg in sorted(self._frames().items()):
+                sig = hashlib.sha1(jpeg).hexdigest()
+                if self._sig.get(role) == sig:
+                    continue                     # a frame judged before is not a new look
+                self._sig[role] = sig
+                ans, _why = self._judge({role: jpeg})
+                if ans is True:
+                    l_yes = 1.0
+                elif ans is False and not l_yes:
+                    l_no = 1.0
+        fast_meas: dict[str, float | None] = {"illegal_grip": 1.0 if l_yes else (0.0 if l_no else None)}
+        if self._grippers is not None and self.ticks % 4 == 0:
+            g = self._grippers() or {}
+            closed = [a.get("opening") for a in g.values() if isinstance(a, dict) and a.get("opening") is not None]
+            if closed:
+                fast_meas["holding"] = 1.0 if min(closed) < 0.15 else 0.0
+        with self._lock:
+            self.obs.fast(fast_meas)
+            u = self.ss.u(dt=dt, l_guard_yes=l_yes, l_guard_no=l_no)
+            d = self.ss.step(u, self.obs.z)
+            for v in pending:
+                rise = self.ss.note_progress(v["progress"]) if v["progress"] is not None else False
+                self.obs.slow({"progress": v["progress"], "illegal_grip": None if v["guard"] is None else
+                               float(v["guard"]), "holding": None if v["holding"] is None else float(v["holding"])})
+                u = self.ss.u(dt=0.0, v_new=1, v_done=v["done"] is True, v_same=v["same"], v_failing=v["failing"],
+                              v_stalled=v["stalled"], v_rise=rise, v_guard_yes=v["guard"] is True,
+                              v_guard_no=v["guard"] is False)
+                d2 = self.ss.step(u, self.obs.z)
+                d = {k: d[k] | d2[k] for k in d}
+            dec = {k: bool(v[0]) for k, v in d.items()}
+            if any(dec.values()) and dec != self.decision:
+                self._log("fast_decision", stage=stage, mode=self.mode, **dec,
+                          z={k: round(float(x), 2) for k, x in zip(("progress", "holding", "at_goal", "illegal",
+                                                                     "cable_out"), self.obs.z)})
+            self.decision = {k: self.decision.get(k, False) or v for k, v in dec.items()}
+            trip = self.mode == "live" and dec["guard"] and self.tripped is None
+            if trip:
+                self.tripped = "fast loop guard"
+        if trip and self._stop is not None:
+            try:
+                self._stop()
+                self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, source="fast", stop="sent")
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+                self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, source="fast", stop=f"failed: {exc}")
+        self.ticks += 1
+        self.tick_ms.append(1000 * (self._clock() - t0))
+        if len(self.tick_ms) > 2000:
+            del self.tick_ms[:1000]
+        return dec
+
+    def start(self) -> None:
+        if self._thread is None:
+            self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._loop, name="fast-loop", daemon=True)
+            self._thread.start()
+
+    def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closed.set()
+        if self._thread is not None:
+            self._thread.join(timeout=2.0)
+
+    def _loop(self) -> None:
+        while not self._closed.is_set():
+            t = self._clock()
+            try:
+                self.step()
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad tick must not kill the loop
+                self._log("fast_loop_error", error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._closed.wait(max(0.0, self.period - (self._clock() - t)))

+"""Hedged calls: send the same request N times at once and use the first good answer.
+
+Model APIs (Claude, Dream-1) have long latency tails: a Haiku 5.5 monitor call took 6.5-13 s for the same request
+(8 Nov). With N copies in flight, the answer arrives at the minimum of N draws instead of one draw, so the tail
+shrinks; the floor (the fastest a call can be) does not move. Cost and rate-limit use grow N-fold.
+
+`first_of` is generic: any function of no arguments that returns a result or raises. The losing calls keep running
+in their daemon threads and their results are dropped (HTTP calls cannot be cancelled mid-flight from here).
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import threading
+import time
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+from typing import Any, Callable, Generic, TypeVar
+
+T = TypeVar("T")
+
+
+@dataclass
+class HedgeResult(Generic[T]):
+    value: T | None
+    winner: int | None              # which copy answered first (0-based), None if none was good
+    latency_s: float
+    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    answered: int = 0               # copies that had finished when the winner was taken
+
+
+def first_of(fn: Callable[[], T], n: int, *, timeout_s: float = 60.0,
+             good: Callable[[T], bool] | None = None, stagger_s: float = 0.0,
+             clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic) -> HedgeResult[T]:
+    """Run `fn` `n` times in parallel; return the first result that `good` accepts (default: any result that did
+    not raise). If every copy fails or is rejected, returns the last good-or-not result with winner None.
+    `stagger_s` delays copy k by k*stagger_s (a smaller extra load when the first copy is usually fast)."""
+    n = max(1, int(n))
+    t0 = clock()
+    if n == 1:
+        try:
+            v = fn()
+            return HedgeResult(v, 0 if good is None or good(v) else None, clock() - t0, [], 1)
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+            return HedgeResult(None, None, clock() - t0, [f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"], 1)
+    cond = threading.Condition()
+    state: dict[str, Any] = {"done": 0, "winner": None, "value": None, "fallback": None, "errors": []}
+
+    def run(k: int) -> None:
+        if stagger_s > 0 and k:
+            time.sleep(k * stagger_s)
+            with cond:
+                if state["winner"] is not None:
+                    return               # someone already answered: don't spend the call
+        try:
+            v = fn()
+            ok = good is None or good(v)
+            err = None
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+            v, ok, err = None, False, f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
+        with cond:
+            state["done"] += 1
+            if err:
+                state["errors"].append(err)
+            elif not ok and state["fallback"] is None:
+                state["fallback"] = v
+            if ok and state["winner"] is None:
+                state["winner"], state["value"] = k, v
+            cond.notify_all()
+
+    for k in range(n):
+        threading.Thread(target=run, args=(k,), name=f"hedge-{k}", daemon=True).start()
+    deadline = t0 + timeout_s
+    with cond:
+        while state["winner"] is None and state["done"] < n:
+            left = deadline - clock()
+            if left <= 0:
+                break
+            cond.wait(min(left, 0.05))
+        w = state["winner"]
+        value = state["value"] if w is not None else state["fallback"]
+        return HedgeResult(value, w, clock() - t0, list(state["errors"]), state["done"])

+"""Illegal-ball guard on live camera frames, in its own thread.
+
+The plan guard otherwise looks at debug-view trips (one every ~3.4 s on the station) once per poll (check_every_s,
+3 s in v4), so a held ball could be up to ~6 s old when judged, and pick-to-bin takes a few seconds. The station
+add-on (station_addons/sitecustomize.py) serves the controller's newest frame at GET /frame/<camera>.jpg, live at
+30 fps whether or not a run is going (checked read-only on the NUC, 7 Nov: 4 ms per frame). This thread runs the
+local detector's guard on each wrist camera at `rate_hz`; `confirm_frames` yes answers in a row on new frames of the
+same camera POST /stop at once, without waiting for the runner's next poll. The runner then ends the match as for
+any guard trip.
+
+Reads only GET /frame; the one thing it sends is /stop, and only while armed (a stage the plan guard covers).
+
+Safety rules (from the Codex review of the first version):
+- The guard never waits on the runner: it sets `tripped` and sends /stop at once. The runner checks `tripped` before
+  each /start, /prompt or /home and again after it; a trip it finds afterwards may have been overtaken by its own
+  command, so it sends /stop again (Runner._motion).
+- Arming has a generation: a judgment that started before a disarm (or a re-arm for another stage) is dropped.
+- Only a new frame counts as a look: the same bytes judged again neither add a yes nor refresh `last_answer_t`, so a
+  frozen camera shows up as blindness (plan guard max_blind_s), not as "all clear".
+- Yes answers are counted per camera, so motion in one wrist view can't confirm a frozen yes in the other.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import hashlib
+import threading
+from concurrent.futures import ALL_COMPLETED, Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, wait
+import time
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+from collections.abc import Callable
+from typing import Any
+
+
+class LiveFrames:
+    """GET /frame/<role>.jpg from the station API for each role, all roles at once (one slow or dead camera must not
+    hold up the others: 4 ms per frame on the NUC, so `timeout_s` is short). A role that fails this tick is missing
+    from the result and is tried again next tick (a camera that recovers is used again)."""
+
+    def __init__(self, base_url: str = "http://127.0.0.1:8091", roles: tuple[str, ...] = ("wrist_l", "wrist_r"),
+                 *, timeout_s: float = 0.25):
+        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip("/")
+        self.roles = list(roles)
+        self.timeout_s = timeout_s
+        self.last_error: str | None = None
+        self._pool = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max(1, len(self.roles)), thread_name_prefix="live-frame")
+        self._pending: dict[str, Future] = {}
+
+    def _one(self, role: str) -> bytes | None:
+        try:
+            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{self.base_url}/frame/{role}.jpg", timeout=self.timeout_s) as resp:
+                return resp.read()
+        except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:
+            self.last_error = f"{role}: HTTP {exc.code}"
+        except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError, TimeoutError) as exc:
+            self.last_error = f"{role}: {exc}"
+        return None
+
+    def grab(self, wait_s: float = 0.05) -> dict[str, bytes]:
+        """The frames that are in within `wait_s`. A camera whose fetch is still running is not waited for: its
+        fetch keeps going and its frame is returned by a later grab, so a dead camera never slows a live one."""
+        for role in self.roles:
+            if role not in self._pending:
+                self._pending[role] = self._pool.submit(self._one, role)
+        wait(list(self._pending.values()), timeout=wait_s, return_when=ALL_COMPLETED)
+        out: dict[str, bytes] = {}
+        for role, job in list(self._pending.items()):
+            if job.done():
+                del self._pending[role]
+                data = job.result()
+                if data is not None:
+                    out[role] = data
+        return out
+
+    def probe(self, tries: int = 3) -> list[str]:
+        """Roles that returned a frame in at least one of `tries` grabs (startup check before relying on them)."""
+        seen: set[str] = set()
+        for _ in range(tries):
+            seen |= set(self.grab(wait_s=self.timeout_s + 0.1))   # let each fetch finish or time out
+            if len(seen) == len(self.roles):
+                break
+        return [r for r in self.roles if r in seen]
+
+
+class LiveGuard:
+    """Runs `judge({role: jpeg})` per camera at `rate_hz` while armed. `judge` returns (True/False/None, why) like
+    TableDetector.guard. `confirm_frames` yes answers in a row on new frames of one camera call `stop()` once and
+    set `tripped` to the reason. A no on new frames of that camera resets its count; None leaves it alone."""
+
+    def __init__(self, source: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]],
+                 judge: Callable[[dict[str, bytes]], tuple[bool | None, str]],
+                 stop: Callable[[], Any], *, rate_hz: float = 10.0, confirm_frames: int = 3,
+                 log: Callable[..., None] | None = None, clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
+        if confirm_frames < 1 or rate_hz <= 0:
+            raise ValueError("confirm_frames must be >= 1 and rate_hz > 0")
+        self.source, self.judge, self._stop = source, judge, stop
+        self.period = 1.0 / rate_hz
+        self.confirm_frames = confirm_frames
+        self._log = log or (lambda *a, **k: None)
+        self._clock = clock
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()           # guards the fields below
+        self._armed: str | None = None          # the covered stage's name while armed
+        self._gen = 0                           # bumped on every arm-from-disarmed and every disarm
+        self._count: dict[str, int] = {}        # per camera: yes answers in a row on new frames
+        self._sig: dict[str, str] = {}          # per camera: signature of the last frame judged
+        self.tripped: str | None = None
+        self.last_answer_t: float | None = None   # clock() of the last yes/no answer on a new frame
+        self.ticks = 0
+        self._closed = threading.Event()
+        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None
+
+    # -- control -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def arm(self, stage: str) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._armed is None:
+                self._gen += 1
+                self._count, self._sig = {}, {}
+                self.last_answer_t = self._clock()   # max_blind_s counts from arming, as the poll guard does
+                self._log("live_guard_armed", stage=stage)
+            self._armed = stage
+
+    @property
+    def armed(self) -> bool:
+        return self._armed is not None
+
+    def disarm(self) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._armed is not None:
+                self._log("live_guard_disarmed", stage=self._armed, ticks=self.ticks)
+            self._armed = None
+            self._gen += 1
+
+    def start(self) -> None:
+        if self._thread is None:
+            self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._loop, name="live-guard", daemon=True)
+            self._thread.start()
+
+    def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closed.set()
+        self.disarm()
+        if self._thread is not None:
+            self._thread.join(timeout=2.0)
+
+    # -- one look ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def step(self) -> None:
+        """One tick: grab, judge each camera, count, maybe stop. The thread calls this; tests call it directly."""
+        with self._lock:
+            stage, gen = self._armed, self._gen
+        if stage is None or self.tripped is not None:
+            return
+        frames = self.source()
+        self.ticks += 1
+        for role in sorted(frames):
+            jpeg = frames[role]
+            sig = hashlib.sha1(jpeg).hexdigest()
+            with self._lock:
+                if gen != self._gen:
+                    return                  # disarmed or re-armed while looking: these answers are stale
+                if sig == self._sig.get(role):
+                    continue                # the same frame again is not a new look
+            answer, why = self.judge({role: jpeg})
+            with self._lock:
+                if gen != self._gen:
+                    return
+                self._sig[role] = sig
+                if answer is None:
+                    continue
+                self.last_answer_t = self._clock()
+                if answer is False:
+                    self._count[role] = 0
+                    continue
+                self._count[role] = count = self._count.get(role, 0) + 1
+            self._log("guard_yes", stage=stage, count=count, reason=why, camera=role, source="live")
+            if count >= self.confirm_frames:
+                self._trip(stage, gen, why)
+                return
+
+    def _trip(self, stage: str, gen: int, why: str) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if gen != self._gen or self.tripped is not None:
+                return
+            self.tripped = why   # set before /stop: the runner sends nothing that moves an arm from here on
+        try:
+            self._stop()
+            self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, reason=why, source="live", stop="sent")
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - the runner's own stop follows on its next check
+            self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, reason=why, source="live", stop=f"failed: {exc}")
+
+    def _loop(self) -> None:
+        while not self._closed.is_set():
+            t = self._clock()
+            try:
+                self.step()
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad tick must not kill the guard
+                self._log("live_guard_error", error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._closed.wait(max(0.0, self.period - (self._clock() - t)))

new file mode 100644

+"""Read-only LiveKit camera observer for Hill Climb practice and evaluation."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import asyncio
+import io
+import logging
+import math
+import os
+import time
+from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass
+from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
+
+from .log import RunLog
+from .plan import Stage, load_plan
+from .verifiers import MonitorVerifier
+
+if TYPE_CHECKING:
+    from livekit import rtc
+
+# Ported from codex/hill-climb-livekit (569607e) without that branch's plan changes, so the camera-name helper
+# lives here and a stage without required_cameras needs none.
+_ROLE_NAMES = {
+    "scene": "overhead", "top": "overhead", "overhead": "overhead",
+    "left": "wrist_l", "wrist_l": "wrist_l",
+    "right": "wrist_r", "wrist_r": "wrist_r",
+}
+
+
+def camera_matches(role: str, wanted: str) -> bool:
+    """Match YAM station and simulator names, or a custom camera role substring."""
+    actual = role.removeprefix("video.").removeprefix("image_window.")
+    expected = wanted.removeprefix("video.").removeprefix("image_window.")
+    canonical = _ROLE_NAMES.get(expected)
+    return wanted in role or (canonical is not None and _ROLE_NAMES.get(actual) == canonical)
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class ReceivedFrame:
+    event: rtc.VideoFrameEvent
+    received_at: float
+
+    def jpeg(self) -> bytes:
+        from PIL import Image
+
+        frame = self.event.frame
+        image = Image.frombytes("RGB", (frame.width, frame.height), bytes(frame.data))
+        # The SDK enumerates clockwise rotation in quarter turns.
+        if self.event.rotation:
+            image = image.rotate(-90 * int(self.event.rotation), expand=True)
+        buffer = io.BytesIO()
+        image.save(buffer, format="JPEG", quality=85)
+        return buffer.getvalue()
+
+
+class LiveKitCameras:
+    """Keep only the latest frame for each explicitly named camera."""
+
+    def __init__(self, publisher: str, tracks: dict[str, str]):
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        self.room = rtc.Room()
+        self.publisher = publisher
+        self._roles = {name: role for role, name in tracks.items()}
+        self._latest: dict[str, ReceivedFrame] = {}
+        self._streams: dict[str, tuple[str, asyncio.Task]] = {}
+        self._tasks: set[asyncio.Task] = set()
+        self._reconnecting = False
+        self._closing = False
+        self.closed = False
+        self.room.on("track_published", self._subscribe)
+        self.room.on("track_subscribed", self._start_stream)
+        self.room.on("track_unsubscribed", self._end_stream)
+        self.room.on("track_muted", self._mute)
+        self.room.on("reconnecting", self._pause)
+        self.room.on("reconnected", self._resume)
+        self.room.on("disconnected", self._disconnected)
+
+    async def connect(self, url: str, token: str) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        await self.room.connect(url, token, rtc.RoomOptions(auto_subscribe=False, connect_timeout=10))
+        for participant in self.room.remote_participants.values():
+            for publication in participant.track_publications.values():
+                self._subscribe(publication, participant)
+
+    def _subscribe(self, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication, participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        if (participant.identity == self.publisher and publication.name in self._roles
+                and publication.kind == rtc.TrackKind.KIND_VIDEO):
+            publication.set_subscribed(True)
+
+    def _start_stream(self, track: rtc.RemoteVideoTrack, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication,
+                      participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        if participant.identity != self.publisher or role is None:
+            return
+        previous = self._streams.get(role)
+        if previous is not None:
+            sid, task = previous
+            if sid == publication.sid and not task.done():
+                return
+            task.cancel()
+        self._latest.pop(role, None)
+        task = asyncio.create_task(self._receive(role, track, publication))
+        self._streams[role] = (publication.sid, task)
+        self._tasks.add(task)
+        task.add_done_callback(self._stream_done)
+
+    def _stream_done(self, task: asyncio.Task) -> None:
+        self._tasks.discard(task)
+        if not task.cancelled() and (error := task.exception()) is not None:
+            logging.getLogger(__name__).warning("LiveKit video stream failed (%s)", type(error).__name__)
+
+    async def _receive(self, role: str, track: rtc.RemoteVideoTrack,
+                       publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        stream = rtc.VideoStream.from_track(track=track, capacity=1, format=rtc.VideoBufferType.RGB24)
+        owner = (publication.sid, asyncio.current_task())
+        try:
+            async for event in stream:
+                if (self._streams.get(role) == owner and not publication.muted
+                        and not self._reconnecting and self.room.isconnected()):
+                    self._latest[role] = ReceivedFrame(event, time.monotonic())
+        finally:
+            if self._streams.get(role) == owner:
+                self._streams.pop(role)
+                self._latest.pop(role, None)
+            await stream.aclose()
+
+    def _end_stream(self, track: rtc.RemoteTrack, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication,
+                    participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        active = self._streams.get(role) if role is not None else None
+        if active is not None and active[0] == publication.sid:
+            _, task = self._streams.pop(role)
+            self._latest.pop(role, None)
+            task.cancel()
+
+    def _mute(self, participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        active = self._streams.get(role) if role is not None else None
+        if active is not None and active[0] == publication.sid:
+            self._latest.pop(role, None)
+
+    def _pause(self) -> None:
+        self._reconnecting = True
+        self._latest.clear()
+
+    def _resume(self) -> None:
+        self._reconnecting = False
+
+    def _disconnected(self, reason: object) -> None:
+        self._latest.clear()
+        self.closed = not self._closing
+
+    def sample(self, now: float, max_age: float, previous: dict[str, ReceivedFrame]) -> dict[str, ReceivedFrame]:
+        """Return a complete set only when each view is fresh and new to the caller."""
+        if self._reconnecting or not self.room.isconnected() or len(self._latest) != len(self._roles):
+            return {}
+        if any(now - frame.received_at > max_age or frame is previous.get(role)
+               for role, frame in self._latest.items()):
+            return {}
+        return dict(self._latest)
+
+    async def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closing = True
+        tasks = tuple(self._tasks)
+        for task in tasks:
+            if not task.done() and not task.cancelling():
+                task.cancel()
+        await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
+        if self.room.isconnected():
+            await self.room.disconnect()
+
+
+async def observe(publisher: str, tracks: dict[str, str], url: str, token: str, log: RunLog, *,
+                  duration: float, interval: float, max_age: float, monitor: MonitorVerifier | None,
+                  stage: Stage | None) -> tuple[int, bool]:
+    cameras = LiveKitCameras(publisher, tracks)
+    previous: dict[str, ReceivedFrame] = {}
+    samples = 0
+    try:
+        await cameras.connect(url, token)
+        started = next_sample = time.monotonic()
+        end = started + duration
+        while time.monotonic() < end and not cameras.closed:
+            now = time.monotonic()
+            current = cameras.sample(now, max_age, previous) if now >= next_sample else {}
+            if not current:
+                await asyncio.sleep(min(0.1, end - now))
+                continue
+            samples += 1
+            previous = current
+            next_sample = now + interval
+            frames = {role: frame.jpeg() for role, frame in current.items()}
+            files = log.save_frames("livekit", frames)
+            log.event("livekit_sample", sample=samples, frames=[file.name for file in files],
+                      cameras={role: {"rtc_timestamp_us": frame.event.timestamp_us,
+                                      "receive_age_ms": round(1000 * (now - frame.received_at), 1)}
+                               for role, frame in current.items()})
+            if monitor is not None and stage is not None:
+                request_started = time.monotonic()
+                verdict = await asyncio.to_thread(monitor.check_now, stage, frames, now - started)
+                log.event("livekit_verdict", sample=samples, stage=stage.name,
+                          request_ms=round(1000 * (time.monotonic() - request_started), 1), **asdict(verdict))
+        return samples, cameras.closed
+    finally:
+        await cameras.close()
+
+
+def positive_seconds(value: str) -> float:
+    number = float(value)
+    if not math.isfinite(number) or number <= 0:
+        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("expected a finite positive number")
+    return number
+
+
+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
+    parser.add_argument("--publisher", required=True, help="LiveKit participant identity that owns the cameras")
+    parser.add_argument("--track", action="append", required=True, metavar="ROLE=NAME",
+                        help="published video track name for overhead, wrist_l or wrist_r")
+    parser.add_argument("--seconds", type=positive_seconds, default=30, help="observation window after connecting")
+    parser.add_argument("--interval", type=positive_seconds, default=3, help="minimum seconds between samples")
+    parser.add_argument("--max-age", type=positive_seconds, default=1, help="max seconds since local frame receipt")
+    parser.add_argument("--runs-dir", default="runs_livekit")
+    parser.add_argument("--plan", help="optional harness YAML for an advisory verdict")
+    parser.add_argument("--stage", help="stage to assess in the observer")
+    parser.add_argument("--var", action="append", default=[], metavar="NAME=VALUE")
+    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
+    tracks = {}
+    for item in args.track:
+        role, _, name = item.partition("=")
+        if role not in ("overhead", "wrist_l", "wrist_r") or not name or role in tracks or name in tracks.values():
+            parser.error("--track needs a unique overhead, wrist_l or wrist_r role and a unique track name")
+        tracks[role] = name
+    if bool(args.plan) != bool(args.stage):
+        parser.error("--plan and --stage must be used together")
+    url, token = os.getenv("LIVEKIT_URL"), os.getenv("LIVEKIT_TOKEN")
+    if not url or not token:
+        parser.error("set LIVEKIT_URL and LIVEKIT_TOKEN")
+    try:
+        from livekit import rtc
+        from PIL import Image
+    except ImportError:
+        parser.error("install livekit and pillow to use the observer")
+    monitor, stage = None, None
+    if args.plan:
+        variables = {}
+        for item in args.var:
+            name, _, value = item.partition("=")
+            if not name or not value:
+                parser.error("--var expects NAME=VALUE")
+            variables[name] = value
+        try:
+            plan = load_plan(args.plan, variables)
+            stage = next((stage for stage in plan.stages if stage.name == args.stage), None)
+            if stage is None:
+                parser.error(f"unknown stage {args.stage!r}")
+            missing = [role for role in getattr(stage, "required_cameras", []) if not any(camera_matches(r, role) for r in tracks)]
+            if missing:
+                parser.error(f"stage requires camera roles {', '.join(missing)}")
+            monitor = MonitorVerifier(plan, history_frames=2, timeout_s=5)
+        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+            parser.error(str(exc))
+    log = RunLog(args.runs_dir)
+    log.update_manifest(source="livekit", publisher=args.publisher, tracks=tracks, stage=args.stage)
+    try:
+        samples, disconnected = asyncio.run(observe(args.publisher, tracks, url, token, log, duration=args.seconds,
+                                                    interval=args.interval, max_age=args.max_age,
+                                                    monitor=monitor, stage=stage))
+    except KeyboardInterrupt:
+        print(f"Observer stopped. Logs at {log.path}")
+        return 130
+    except (rtc.ConnectError, TimeoutError) as exc:
+        log.event("livekit_connect_failed", error_type=type(exc).__name__)
+        print(f"LiveKit connection failed. Logs at {log.path}")
+        return 1
+    log.write_summary({"samples": samples, "disconnected": disconnected})
+    print(f"Saved {samples} LiveKit camera sets to {log.path}")
+    return 0 if samples and not disconnected else 1
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

index 0000000..1d6b239
